# wf-b s01

Started: 2026-10-05T02:39:41.549999+00:00

## User prompt

```text
Goal: replace SQLAlchemy with Tortoise ORM across the Mealie backend.

Fixed decisions:
- Use Tortoise ORM, which is async. When the rewrite is finished, nothing under mealie/
  should import SQLAlchemy.
- Keep support for both SQLite and PostgreSQL.
- Replace Alembic with Aerich. Upgrading existing databases from the old Alembic history
  is out of scope.
- Keep the HTTP API and the Pydantic schemas in mealie/schema unchanged, so the frontend
  does not need to change.
- Database access becomes async. Make routes, services and scheduler tasks async where
  they need to be.
- You may change how the tests set up and access the database (tests/conftest.py,
  tests/fixtures/, and database setup code inside test files). You must not delete tests,
  skip them, mark them xfail, remove or weaken assertions, or change expected values.
- Run the tests with: uv run pytest -n 4 --dist loadfile
  (tests in the same file depend on each other, so they must run grouped by file).
- Do not push, and do not change git remotes or git configuration.

Do not change any code in this session. Read the backend (mealie/db, mealie/repos,
mealie/services, mealie/routes, mealie/schema, mealie/alembic, tests/conftest.py and
tests/fixtures) and write a migration plan to REWRITE_PLAN.md in the repository root.

The plan must:
1. List every place that depends on SQLAlchemy, grouped by package.
2. Split the work into stages, each small enough to finish in one session, in the order
   they should be done.
3. Give each stage acceptance criteria: what must be true, and which test results show it.
4. List the risks you expect, including SQLite and PostgreSQL differences.
```

## agent_message at 2026-10-05T02:39:48.123855+00:00

I’ll inventory the backend’s SQLAlchemy dependencies and trace where async access will affect callers, then write a staged migration plan in `REWRITE_PLAN.md`. I’ll keep all code unchanged.

  "aggregated_output": "# Mealie Development Guide for AI Agents\n\n## Project Overview\n\nMealie is a self-hosted recipe manager, meal planner, and shopping list application with a FastAPI backend (Python 3.14) and Nuxt 4 frontend (Vue 3 + TypeScript). It uses SQLAlchemy ORM with support for SQLite and PostgreSQL databases.\n\n**Development vs Production:**\n- **Development:** Frontend (port 3000) and backend (port 9000) run as separate processes\n- **Production:** Frontend is statically generated and served via FastAPI's SPA module (`mealie/routes/spa/`) in a single container\n\n## Architecture & Key Patterns\n\n### Backend Architecture (mealie/)\n\n**Repository-Service-Controller Pattern:**\n- **Controllers** (`mealie/routes/**/controller_*.py`): Inherit from `BaseUserController` or `BaseAdminController`, handle HTTP concerns, delegate to services\n- **Services** (`mealie/services/`): Business logic layer, inherit from `BaseService`, coordinate repos and external dependencies\n- **Repositories** (`mealie/repos/`): Data access layer using SQLAlchemy, accessed via `AllRepositories` factory\n  - Get repos via dependency injection: `repos: AllRepositories = Depends(get_repositories)`\n  - All repos scoped to group/household context automatically\n\n**Route Organization:**\n- Routes in `mealie/routes/` organized by domain (auth, recipe, groups, households, admin)\n- Use `APIRouter` with FastAPI dependency injection\n- Apply `@router.get/post/put/delete` decorators with Pydantic response models\n- Route controllers use `HttpRepo` mixin for common CRUD operations (see `mealie/routes/_base/mixins.py`)\n\n**Schemas & Type Generation:**\n- Pydantic schemas in `mealie/schema/` with strict separation: `*In`, `*Out`, `*Create`, `*Update` suffixes\n- Auto-exported from submodules via `__init__.py` files (generated by `task dev:generate`)\n- TypeScript types auto-generated from Pydantic schemas - **never manually edit** `frontend/app/lib/api/types/`\n\n**Database & Sessions:**\n- Session management via `Depends(generate_session)` in FastAPI routes\n- Use `session_context()` context manager in services/scripts\n- SQLAlchemy models in `mealie/db/models/`, migrations in `mealie/alembic/`\n- Create migrations: `task py:migrate -- \"description\"`\n\n### Frontend Architecture (frontend/)\n\n**Component Organization (strict naming conventions):**\n- **Domain Components** (`components/Domain/`): Feature-specific, prefix with domain (e.g., `AdminDashboard`)\n- **Global Components** (`components/global/`): Reusable primitives, prefix with `Base` (e.g., `BaseButton`)\n- **Layout Components** (`components/Layout/`): Layout-only, prefix with `App` if props or `The` if singleton\n- **Page Components** (`components/` with page prefix): Last resort for breaking up complex pages\n\n**API Client Pattern:**\n- API clients in `frontend/app/lib/api/` extend `BaseAPI`, `BaseCRUDAPI`, or `BaseCRUDAPIReadOnly`\n- Types imported from auto-generated `frontend/app/lib/api/types/` (DO NOT EDIT MANUALLY)\n- Composables in `frontend/app/composables/` for shared state and API logic (e.g., `use-mealie-auth.ts`)\n- Use `useAuthBackend()` for authentication state, `useMealieAuth()` for user management\n\n**State Management:**\n- Nuxt 4 composables for state (no Vuex)\n- Auth state via `use-mealie-auth.ts` composable\n- Prefer composables over global state stores\n\n## Essential Commands (via Task/Taskfile.yml)\n\n**Development workflow:**\n```bash\ntask setup              # Install all dependencies (Python + Node)\ntask dev:services       # Start Postgres & Mailpit containers\ntask py                 # Start FastAPI backend (port 9000)\ntask ui                 # Start Nuxt frontend (port 3000)\ntask docs               # Start Zensical documentation server\n```\n\n**Code generation (REQUIRED after schema changes):**\n```bash\ntask dev:generate       # Generate TypeScript types, schema exports, test helpers\n```\n\n**Testing & Quality:**\n```bash\ntask py:test            # Run pytest (supports args: task py:test -- -k test_name)\ntask py:check           # Format + lint + type-check + test (full validation)\ntask py:format          # Ruff format\ntask py:lint            # Ruff check\ntask py:mypy            # Type checking\ntask ui:test            # Vitest frontend tests\ntask ui:check           # Frontend lint + test\n```\n\n**Database:**\n```bash\ntask py:migrate -- \"description\"  # Generate Alembic migration\ntask py:postgres        # Run backend with PostgreSQL config\n```\n\n**Docker:**\n```bash\ntask docker:prod        # Build and run production Docker compose\n```\n\n## Critical Development Practices\n\n### Python Backend\n\n1. **Always use `uv` for Python commands** (not `python` or `pip`):\n   ```bash\n   uv run python mealie/app.py\n   uv run pytest tests/\n   ```\n\n2. **Type hints are mandatory:** Use mypy-compatible annotations, handle Optional types explicitly\n\n3. **Dependency injection pattern:**\n   ```python\n   from fastapi import Depends\n   from mealie.repos.all_repositories import get_repositories, AllRepositories\n\n\n   def my_route(repos: AllRepositories = Depends(get_repositories), user: PrivateUser = Depends(get_current_user)):\n       recipe = repos.recipes.get_one(recipe_id)\n   ```\n\n4. **Settings & Configuration:**\n   - Get settings: `settings = get_app_settings()` (cached singleton)\n   - Get directories: `dirs = get_app_dirs()`\n   - Never instantiate `AppSettings()` directly\n\n5. **Testing:**\n   - Fixtures in `tests/fixtures/`\n   - Use `api_client` fixture for integration tests\n   - Follow existing patterns in `tests/integration_tests/` and `tests/unit_tests/`\n\n### Frontend\n\n1. **Run code generation after backend schema changes:** `task dev:generate`\n\n2. **TypeScript strict mode:** All code must pass type checking\n\n3. **Component naming:** Follow strict conventions (see Architecture section above)\n\n4. **API calls pattern:**\n   ```typescript\n   const api = useUserApi();\n   const recipe = await api.recipes.getOne(recipeId);\n   ```\n\n5. **Composables for shared logic:** Prefer composables in `composables/` over inline code duplication\n\n6. **Translations:** Only modify `en-US` locale files when adding new translation strings - other locales are managed via Crowdin and **must never be modified** (PRs modifying non-English locales will be rejected)\n\n### Cross-Cutting Concerns\n\n1. **Code generation is source of truth:** After Pydantic schema changes, run `task dev:generate` to update:\n   - TypeScript types (`frontend/app/lib/api/types/`)\n   - Schema exports (`mealie/schema/*/__init__.py`)\n   - Test data paths and routes\n\n2. **Multi-tenancy:** All data scoped to **groups** and **households**:\n   - Groups contain multiple households\n   - Households contain recipes, meal plans, shopping lists\n   - Repositories automatically filter by group/household context\n\n3. **Pre-commit hooks:** Install via `task setup:py`, enforces Ruff formatting/linting\n\n4. **Testing before PRs:** Run `task py:check` and `task ui:check` before submitting PRs\n\n## Pull Request Best Practices\n\n### Before Submitting a PR\n\n1. **Draft PRs are optional:** Create a draft PR early if you want feedback while working, or open directly as ready when complete\n2. **Verify code generation:** If you modified Pydantic schemas, ensure `task dev:generate` was run\n3. **Follow Conventional Commits:** Title your PR according to the conventional commits format (see PR template)\n4. **Add release notes:** Include user-facing changes in the PR description\n\n### What to Review\n\n**Architecture & Patterns:**\n- Does the code follow the repository-service-controller pattern?\n- Are controllers delegating business logic to services?\n- Are services coordinating repositories, not accessing the database directly?\n- Is dependency injection used properly (`Depends(get_repositories)`, `Depends(get_current_user)`)?\n\n**Data Scoping:**\n- Are repositories correctly scoped to group/household context?\n- Do route handlers properly validate group/household ownership before operations?\n- Are multi-tenant boundaries enforced (users can't access other groups' data)?\n\n**Type Safety:**\n- Are type hints present on all functions and methods?\n- Are Pydantic schemas using correct suffixes (`*In`, `*Out`, `*Create`, `*Update`)?\n- For frontend, does TypeScript code pass strict type checking?\n\n**Generated Files:**\n- Verify `frontend/app/lib/api/types/` files weren't manually edited (they're auto-generated)\n- Check that `mealie/schema/*/__init__.py` exports match actual schema files (auto-generated)\n- If schemas changed, confirm generated files were updated via `task dev:generate`\n\n**Code Quality:**\n- Is the code readable and well-organized?\n- Are complex operations documented with clear comments?\n- Do component names follow the strict naming conventions (Domain/Global/Layout/Page prefixes)?\n- Are composables used for shared frontend logic instead of duplication?\n\n**Translations:**\n- Were only `en-US` locale files modified for new translation strings?\n- Verify no other locale files (managed by Crowdin) were touched\n\n**Database Changes:**\n- Are Alembic migrations included for schema changes?\n- Are migrations tested against both SQLite and PostgreSQL?\n\n### Review Etiquette\n\n- Be constructive and specific in feedback\n- Suggest code examples when proposing changes\n- Focus on architecture and logic - formatting/linting is handled by CI\n- Use \"Approve\" when ready to merge, \"Request Changes\" for blocking issues, \"Comment\" for non-blocking suggestions\n\n## Common Gotchas\n\n- **Don't manually edit generated files:** `frontend/app/lib/api/types/`, schema `__init__.py` files\n- **Repository context:** Repos are group/household-scoped - passing wrong IDs causes 404s\n- **Session handling:** Don't create sessions manually, use dependency injection or `session_context()`\n- **Schema changes require codegen:** After changing Pydantic models, run `task dev:generate`\n- **Translation files:** Only modify `en-US` locale files - all other locales are managed by Crowdin\n- **Dev containers:** This project uses VS Code dev containers - leverage the pre-configured environment\n- **Task commands:** Use `task` commands instead of direct tool invocation for consistency\n\n## Key Files to Reference\n\n- `Taskfile.yml` - All development commands and workflows\n- `mealie/routes/_base/base_controllers.py` - Controller base classes and patterns\n- `mealie/repos/repository_factory.py` - Repository factory and available repos\n- ## agent_message at 2026-10-05T02:42:26.937472+00:00

The largest changes extend beyond the model definitions: nested writes, query filtering, search, backups, authentication, and scheduled jobs all rely on SQLAlchemy behavior. I’ll give these separate stages with test gates. Some tests inspect SQLAlchemy query objects or restore historical backups; the plan will call out how to preserve those checks and where the current constraints need clarification.

UpdatedAtField\nfrom mealie.schema.response.pagination import PaginationBase\n\nfrom ...db.models.recipe import (\n    IngredientFoodModel,\n    IngredientFoodSubstitutionModel,\n    RecipeComment,\n RecipeCommentOut\nfrom .recipe_notes import RecipeNote\nfrom .recipe_nutrition import Nutrition\nfrom .recipe_settings import RecipeSettings\nfrom .recipe_step import RecipeStep\n\napp_dirs = get_app_dirs()\n\n\ndef create_recipe_slug(name: str, max_length: int = 250) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Generate a slug from a recipe name, truncating to a reasonable length.\n\n    Args:\n        name: The recipe name to create a slug from\n        max_length: Maximum length for the slug (default: 250)\n\n    Returns:\n        A truncated slug string\n\n    Raises:\n        ValueError: If the name cannot be converted to a valid slug\n    \"\"\"\n    generated_slug = slugify(name)\n    if not generated_slug:\n        raise SlugError(\"Recipe name cannot be empty or contain only special characters\")\n    if len(generated_slug) > max_length:\n        generated_slug = generated_slug[:max_length]\n    return generated_slug\n\n\nclass RecipeTag(MealieModel):\n    id: UUID4 | None = None\n    group_id: UUID4 | None = None\n    name: str\n    slug: str\n    recipe_count: int = 0\n\n    _searchable_properties: ClassVar[list[str]] = [\"name\"]\n    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)\n\n\nclass RecipeTagPagination(PaginationBase):\n    items: list[RecipeTag]\n\n\nclass RecipeCategory(RecipeTag):\n    pass\n\n\nclass RecipeCategoryPagination(PaginationBase):\n    items: list[RecipeCategory]\n\n\nclass RecipeTool(RecipeTag):\n    id: UUID4\n    households_with_tool: list[str] = []\n\n    @field_validator(\"households_with_tool\", mode=\"before\")\n    def convert_households_to_slugs(cls, v):\n        if not v:\n            return []\n\n        try:\n            return [household.slug for household in v]\n        except AttributeError:\n            return v\n\n\nclass RecipeToolPagination(PaginationBase):\n    items: list[RecipeTool]\n\n\nclass RecipeTagIn(MealieModel):\n    \"\"\"Lenient variant of RecipeTag accepted only on the recipe write path (JSON import/edit),\n    where a client may omit id/slug and expect them to be resolved or generated server-side.\n    Never used for responses -- RecipeTag itself stays strictly required there.\n\n    Deliberately does not subclass RecipeTag: narrowing id/slug from required to optional in a\n    subclass would be an unsound field override (mypy correctly rejects it), so this is built as\n    an independent sibling with the same fields instead, matching how RecipeToolCreate/Save/Out\n    are already kept as separate classes elsewhere in this module rather than narrowing each other.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    id: UUID4 | None = None\n    group_id: UUID4 | None = None\n    name: str\n    slug: str | None = None\n    recipe_count: int = 0\n\n    _searchable_properties: ClassVar[list[str]] = [\"name\"]\n    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)\n\n    def model_post_init(self, __context: Any) -> None:\n        if not self.slug:\n            self.slug = slugify(self.name)\n\n\nclass RecipeCategoryIn(RecipeTagIn):\n\n    @property\n    def recipe_yield_display(self) -> str:\n        # Fall back to recipe_servings when no yield is set at all, otherwise\n        # a servings-only recipe emits a bare \"0.0\" (or \"0.0 None\") into schema.org.\n        quantity = self.recipe_yield_quantity or (self.recipe_servings if not self.recipe_yield else 0)\n        number = f\"{quantity:g}\" if quantity else \"\"\n        return f\"{number} {self.recipe_yield or ''}\".strip()\n\n    @classmethod\n    def loader_options(cls) -> list[LoaderOption]:\n        return [\n            joinedload(RecipeModel.recipe_category),\n            joinedload(RecipeModel.tags),\n            joinedload(RecipeModel.tools),\n            joinedload(RecipeModel.user).load_only(User.household_id),\n        ]\n\n\nclass RecipePagination(PaginationBase):\n    items: list[RecipeSummary]\n\n\nclass Recipe(RecipeSummary):\n    recipe_ingredient: Annotated[list[RecipeIngredient], Field(validate_default=True)] = []\n    recipe_instructions: list[RecipeStep] | None = []\n    nutrition: Nutrition | None = None\n\n    # Mealie Specific\n    settings: RecipeSettings | None = None\n    assets: list[RecipeAsset] | None = []\n    notes: list[RecipeNote] | None = []\n    extras: dict | None = {}\n\n    comments: list[RecipeCommentOut] | None = []\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _get_dir(dir: Path) -> Path:\n        \"\"\"Gets a directory and creates it if it doesn't exist\"\"\"\n\n        dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True, parents=True)\n        return dir\n\n    @classmethod\n    def directory_from_id(cls, recipe_id: UUID4 | str) -> Path:\n        return cls._get_dir(app_dirs.RECIPE_DATA_DIR.joinpath(str(recipe_id)))\n\n    @classmethod\n    def asset_dir_from_id(cls, recipe_id: UUID4 | str) -> Path:\n        return cls._get_dir(cls.directory_from_id(recipe_id).joinpath(\"assets\"))\n\n    def validate_user_id(user_id: Any):\n        if isinstance(user_id, int):\n            return uuid4()\n        return user_id\n\n    @field_validator(\"extras\", mode=\"before\")\n    def convert_extras_to_dict(cls, v):\n        if isinstance(v, dict):\n            return v\n\n        return {x.key_name: x.value for x in v} if v else {}\n\n    @field_validator(\"nutrition\", mode=\"before\")\n    def validate_nutrition(cls, v):\n        return v or None\n\n    @classmethod\n    def loader_options(cls) -> list[LoaderOption]:\n        return [\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.assets),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.comments).joinedload(RecipeComment.user),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.extras),\n            joinedload(RecipeModel.recipe_category),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.tags),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.tools),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.recipe_ingredient).joinedload(RecipeIngredientModel.unit),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.recipe_ingredient)\n            .joinedload(RecipeIngredientModel.food)\n            .joinedload(IngredientFoodModel.extras),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.recipe_ingredient)\n            .joinedload(RecipeIngredientModel.food)\n            .joinedload(IngredientFoodModel.label),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.recipe_ingredient)\n            .joinedload(RecipeIngredientModel.food)\n            .selectinload(IngredientFoodModel.substitutions)\n            .joinedload(IngredientFoodSubstitutionModel.substitute_food),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.recipe_ingredient)\n            .selectinload(RecipeIngredientModel.substitutions)\n            .joinedload(RecipeIngredientSubstitutionModel.substitute_food),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.recipe_instructions).joinedload(RecipeInstruction.ingredient_references),\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.recipe_instructions).joinedload(RecipeInstruction.note_references),\n            joinedload(RecipeModel.nutrition),\n            joinedload(RecipeModel.settings),\n            # for whatever reason, joinedload can mess up the order here, so use selectinload just this once\n            selectinload(RecipeModel.notes),\n        ]\n\n    @classmethod\n    def filter_search_query(\n        cls, db_model, query: Select, session: Session, search_type: SearchType, search: str, search_list: list[str]\n    ) -> Select:\n        \"\"\"\n        1. token search looks for any individual exact hit in name, description, and ingredients\n        2. fuzzy search looks for trigram hits in name, description, and ingredients\n        3. Sort order is determined by closeness to the recipe name\n        Should search also look at tags?\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if search_type is SearchType.fuzzy:\n            # I would prefer to just do this in the recipe_ingredient.any part of the main query,\n            # but it turns out that at least sqlite wont use indexes for that correctly anymore and\n            # takes a big hit, so prefiltering it is\n            ingredient_ids = (\n                session.execute(\n                    select(RecipeIngredientModel.id).filter(\n                        or_(\n                            RecipeIngredientModel.note_normalized.op(\"%>\")(search),\n                            RecipeIngredientModel.original_text_normalized.op(\"%>\")(search),\n                        )\n                    )\n                )\n                .scalars()\n                .all()\n            )\n\n            session.execute(text(f\"set pg_trgm.word_similarity_threshold = {cls._fuzzy_similarity_threshold};\"))\n            return query.filter(\n                or_(\n                    RecipeModel.name_normalized.op(\"%>\")(search),\n                    RecipeModel.description_normalized.op(\"%>\")(search),\n                    RecipeModel.recipe_ingredient.any(RecipeIngredientModel.id.in_(ingredient_ids)),\n                )\n            ).order_by(  # trigram ordering could be too slow on million record db, but is fine with thousands.\n                func.least(\n                    RecipeModel.name_normalized.op(\"<->>\")(search),\n                )\n            )\n\n        else:\n            ingredient_ids = (\n                session.execute(\n                    select(RecipeIngredientModel.id).filter(\n                        or_(\n                            *[RecipeIngredientModel.note_normalized.like(f\"%{ns}%\") for ns in search_list],\n                            *[RecipeIngredientModel.original_text_normalized.like(f\"%{ns}%\") for ns in search_list],\n                        )\n                    )\n                )\n                .scalars()\n                .all()\n            )\n\n            return query.filter(\n                or_(\n                    *[RecipeModel.name_normalized.like(f\"%{ns}%\") for ns in search_list],\n                    *[RecipeModel.description_normalized.like(f\"%{ns}%\") for ns in search_list],\n                    RecipeModel.recipe_ingredient.any(RecipeIngredientModel.id.in_(ingredient_ids)),\n                )\n            ).order_by(desc(RecipeModel.name_normalized.like(f\"%{search}%\")))\n\n\nclass RecipeIn(Recipe):\n    \"\"\"Recipe as accepted on the write path only (create/update/patch request bodies).\n\n    Unlike Recipe -- which is also used for responses and must guarantee real ids/slugs on\n    its organizers -- this allows a client (e.g. hand-edited or re-imported JSON) to submit\n    tags/categories/tools that omit id/slug, to be resolved or generated server-side.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    tags: Annotated[list[RecipeTagIn] | None, Field(validate_default=True)] = []\n    recipe_category: Annotated[list[RecipeCategoryIn] | None, Field(validate_default=True)] = []\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel\n\n\ndef mapper[U: BaseModel, T: BaseModel](source: U, dest: T, **_) -> T:\n    \"\"\"\n    Map a source model to a destination model. Only top-level fields are mapped.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    for field in source.__class__.model_fields:\n        if field in dest.__class__.model_fields:\n            setattr(dest, field, getattr(source, field))\n\n    return dest\n\n\ndef cast[U: BaseModel, T: BaseModel](source: U, dest: type[T], **kwargs) -> T:\n    create_data = {\n        field: getattr(source, field) for field in source.__class__.model_fields if field in dest.model_fields\n    }\n    create_data.update(kwargs or {})\n    return dest(**create_data)\nfrom inspect import signature\n\nfrom fastapi.exceptions import HTTPException, RequestValidationError\nfrom pydantic import ValidationError\n\n\ndef format_exception(ex: Exception) -> str:\n    return f\"{ex.__class__.__name__}: {ex}\"\n\n\ndef make_dependable(cls):\n    \"\"\"\n    Pydantic BaseModels are very powerful because we get lots of validations and type checking right out of the box.\n    FastAPI can accept a BaseModel as a route Dependency and it will automatically handle things like documentation\n    and error handling. However, if we define custom validators then the errors they raise are not handled, leading\n    to HTTP 500's being returned.\n\n    To better understand this issue, you can visit https://github.com/tiangolo/fastapi/issues/1474 for context.\n\n    A workaround proposed there adds a classmethod which attempts to init the BaseModel and handles formatting of\n    any raised ValidationErrors, custom or otherwise. However, this means essentially duplicating the class's\n    signature. This function automates the creation of a workaround method with a matching signature so that you\n    can avoid code duplication.\n\n    usage:\n    async def fetch(thing_request: ThingRequest = Depends(make_dependable(ThingRequest))):\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def init_cls_and_handle_errors(*args, **kwargs):\n        try:\n            signature(init_cls_and_handle_errors).bind(*args, **kwargs)\n            return cls(*args, **kwargs)\n        except (ValidationError, RequestValidationError) as e:\n            for error in e.errors():\n                error[\"loc\"] = [\"query\", *list(error[\"loc\"])]\n            raise HTTPException(422, detail=[format_exception(ex) for ex in e.errors()]) from None\n\n    init_cls_and_handle_errors.__signature__ = signature(cls)\n    return DetermineSecretsTests:\n    def test_non_production_returns_fixed_key(self, tmp_path: Path):\n        result = determine_secrets(tmp_path, \".secret\", production=False)\n        assert result == result = determine_secrets(tmp_path, \".secret\", production=True)\n        assert result == \"existing-secret\"\n\n    def test_regenerates_when_file_is_empty(self, tmp_path: Path):\n        (tmp_path / \".secret\").write_text(\"\")\n        result = determine_secrets(tmp_path, \".secret\", production=True)\n        assert result\n        assert (tmp_path / \".secret\").read_text() == result\n\n    def test_regenerates_when_file_is_whitespace_only(self, tmp_path: Path):\n        (tmp_path / \".secret\").write_text(\"   \\n  \")\n        result = determine_secrets(tmp_path, \".secret\", production=True)\n        assert result\n        assert (tmp_path / \".secret\").read_text() == result\n\n    def test_generates_unique_secrets(self, tmp_path: Path):\n        dir_a = tmp_path / \"a\"\n        dir_b = tmp_path / \"b\"\n        result_a = determine_secrets(dir_a, \".secret\", production=True)\n        result_b = determine_secrets(dir_b, \".secret\", production=True)\n        assert result_a != result_b\n\n    def test_no_tmp_file_left_after_write(self, tmp_path: Path):\n        determine_secrets(tmp_path, \".secret\", production=True)\n import create_model\nfrom pydantic2ts.cli.script import generate_json_schema_v2\n\nCODE_GENERATION_DIR = Path(__file__).parents[2] / \"dev\" / \"code-generation\"\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef gen_ts_types(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> Iterator[ModuleType]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Import the code generation script without leaking its top-level `utils` package into\n    `sys.modules`, where it could shadow other imports for the rest of the test session\n    \"\"\"\n    monkeypatch.syspath_prepend(str(CODE_GENERATION_DIR))\n    try:\n        yield importlib.import_module(\"gen_ts_types\")\n    finally:\n        for name in list(sys.modules):\n            if name in (\"gen_ts_types\", \"utils\") or name.startswith(\"utils.\"):\n                del sys.modules[name]\n\n\ndef test_type_generation_restores_model_config(gen_ts_types: ModuleType) -> None:\n    model = create_model(\"UnconfiguredModel\", value=(str, ...))\n\n    with gen_ts_types.forbid_extra_fields([model]):\n        assert model.model_config[\"extra\"] == \"forbid\"\n        generate_json_schema_v2([model])\n\n    assert model.model_config == {}\nfrom inspect\nimport time\nfrom functools import cached_property\nfrom uuid import uuid4\n\nimport pytest\nfrom pydantic import UUID4\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom mealie.schema.response.pagination import PaginationQuery\nfrom mealie.services.household_services.shopping_lists import ShoppingListService\nfrom tests.utils.factories import random_email, random_string\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"group_id\", [uuid4(), None, NOT_SET])\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"household_id\", [uuid4(), None, NOT_SET])\ndef test_get_repositories_sets_ids(\n    session: Session, group_id: UUID4 | None | NotSet, household_id: UUID4 | None | NotSet\n):\n    kwargs = {}\n    if not isinstance(group_id, NotSet):\n        kwargs[\"group_id\"] = group_id\n    if not isinstance(household_id, NotSet):\n        kwargs[\"household_id\"] = household_id\n\n    repositories = get_repositories(session, **kwargs)\n    assert repositories.group_id == group_id\n    assert repositories.household_id == household_id\n\n    # test that sentinel is used correctly\n    if isinstance(group_id, NotSet):\n        assert repositories.group_id is NOT_SET\n    if isinstance(household_id, NotSet):\n        assert repositories.household_id is NOT_SET\n\n\ndef test_repository_generic_constructor(session: Session):\n    RepositoryGeneric(session, \"id\", MealieModel, SqlAlchemyBase)\n\n\ndef test_repository_group_constructor(session: Session):\n    BASE_ARGS = (session, \"id\", MealieModel, SqlAlchemyBase)\n\n    with pytest.raises(ValueError):\n        GroupRepositoryGeneric(*BASE_ARGS, group_id=NOT_SET)\n\n    GroupRepositoryGeneric(*BASE_ARGS, group_id=None)\n    GroupRepositoryGeneric(*BASE_ARGS, group_id=uuid4())\n\n\ndef test_repository_household_constructor(session: Session):\n    BASE_ARGS = (session, \"id\", MealieModel, SqlAlchemyBase)\n\n    with pytest.raises(ValueError):\n HouseholdRepositoryGeneric(*BASE_ARGS, group_id=NOT_SET, household_id=uuid4())\n\n    HouseholdRepositoryGeneric(*BASE_ARGS, group_id=None, household_id=None)\n    HouseholdRepositoryGeneric(*BASE_ARGS, group_id=uuid4(), household_id=None)\n    HouseholdRepositoryGeneric(*BASE_ARGS, group_id=None, household_id=uuid4())\n    HouseholdRepositoryGeneric(*BASE_ARGS, group_id=uuid4(), household_id=uuid4())\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"use_group_id\", [True, False])\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"use_household_id\", [True, False])\ndef test_all_repositories_constructors(session: Session, use_group_id: bool, use_household_id: bool):\n    kwargs = {}\n    if use_group_id:\n        kwargs[\"group_id\"] = uuid4()\n    if use_household_id:\n        kwargs[\"household_id\"] = uuid4()\n    repositories = get_repositories(session, **kwargs)\n    for name, member in inspect.getmembers(repositories.__class__):\n        if not isinstance(member, cached_property):\n            continue\n        signature = inspect.signature(member.func)\n        repo_type = signature.return_annotation\n        try:\n            if not issubclass(repo_type, RepositoryGeneric):\n                continue\n        except TypeError:\n            continue\n\n        if issubclass(repo_type, HouseholdRepositoryGeneric):\n            if not (use_group_id and use_household_id):\n                with pytest.raises(ValueError):\n                    getattr(repositories, name)\n            else:\n                repo = getattr(repositories, name)\n                assert repo.group_id == kwargs[\"group_id\"]\n                assert repo.household_id == kwargs[\"household_id\"]\n        elif issubclass(repo_type, GroupRepositoryGeneric):\n            if not use_group_id:\n                with pytest.raises(ValueError):\n                    getattr(repositories, name)\n            else:\n                repo = getattr(repositories, name)\n                assert repo.group_id == kwargs[\"group_id\"]\n                assert repo.household_id is None\n        else:\n            repo = getattr(repositories, name)\n            assert repo.group_id is None\n            assert repo.household_id is None\n\n\ndef test_group_repositories_filter_by_group(session: Session):\n    unfiltered_repos = get_repositories(session, group_id=None, household_id=None)\n    group_1 = unfiltered_repos.groups.create({\"name\": random_string()})\n    group_2 = unfiltered_repos.groups.create({\"name\": random_string()})\n\n    group_1_repos = get_repositories(session, group_id=group_1.id, household_id=None)\n    group_2_repos = get_repositories(session, group_id=group_2.id, household_id=None)\n    food_1 = group_1_repos.ingredient_foods.create(\n        SaveIngredientFood(id=uuid4(), group_id=group_1.id, name=random_string())\n    )\n    food_2 = group_2_repos.ingredient_foods.create(\n        SaveIngredientFood(id=uuid4(), group_id=group_2.id, name=random_string())\n    )\n\n    # unfiltered_repos should find both foods\n    assert food_1 == unfiltered_repos.ingredient_foods.get_one(food_1.id)\n    assert food_2 == unfiltered_repos.ingredient_foods.get_one(food_2.id)\n    all_foods = unfiltered_repos.ingredient_foods.page_all(PaginationQuery(page=1, per_page=-1)).items\n    assert food_1 in all_foods\n    assert food_2 in all_foods\n\n    # group_repos should only find foods with the correct group_id\n    assert food_1 == group_1_repos.ingredient_foods.get_one(food_1.id)\n    assert group_1_repos.ingredient_foods.get_one(food_2.id) is None\n    assert [food_1] == group_1_repos.ingredient_foods.page_all(PaginationQuery(page=1, per_page=-1)).items\n\n    assert group_2_repos.ingredient_foods.get_one(food_1.id) is None\n    assert food_2 == group_2_repos.ingredient_foods.get_one(food_2.id)\n    assert [food_2] == group_2_repos.ingredient_foods.page_all(PaginationQuery(page=1, per_page=-1)).items\n\n\ndef test_household_repositories_filter_by_household(session: Session):\n    unfiltered_repos = get_repositories(session, group_id=None, household_id=None)\n    group = unfiltered_repos.groups.create({\"name\": random_string()})\n    group_repos = get_repositories(session, group_id=group.id, household_id=None)\n    household_1 = group_repos.households.create({\"name\": random_string(), \"group_id\": group.id})\n    household_2 = group_repos.households.create({\"name\": random_string(), \"group_id\": group.id})\n\n    household_1_repos = get_repositories(session, group_id=group.id, household_id=household_1.id)\n    household_2_repos = get_repositories(session, group_id=group.id, household_id=household_2.id)\n    webhook_1 = household_1_repos.webhooks.create(\n        SaveWebhook(group_id=group.id, household_id=household_1.id, scheduled_time=time.time())\n    )\n    webhook_2 = household_2_repos.webhooks.create(\n        SaveWebhook(group_id=group.id, household_id=household_2.id, scheduled_time=time.time())\n    )\n\n    # unfiltered_repos and group_repos should find both webhooks\n    for repos in [unfiltered_repos, group_repos]:\n        assert webhook_1 == repos.webhooks.get_one(webhook_1.id)\n        assert webhook_2 == repos.webhooks.get_one(webhook_2.id)\n        all_webhooks = repos.webhooks.page_all(PaginationQuery(page=1, per_page=-1)).items\n        assert webhook_1 in all_webhooks\n        assert webhook_2 in all_webhooks\n\n    # household_repos should only find webhooks with the correct household_id\n    assert webhook_1 == household_1_repos.webhooks.get_one(webhook_1.id)\n    assert household_1_repos.webhooks.get_one(webhook_2.id) is None\n    assert [webhook_1] == household_1_repos.webhooks.page_all(PaginationQuery(page=1, per_page=-1)).items\n\n    assert household_2_repos.webhooks.get_one(webhook_1.id) is None\n    assert webhook_2 == household_2_repos.webhooks.get_one(webhook_2.id)\n    assert [webhook_2] == household_2_repos.webhooks.page_all(PaginationQuery(page=1, per_page=-1)).items\n\n    # a different group's repos shouldn't find anything\n    other_group = unfiltered_repos.groups.create({\"name\": random_string()})\n    for household_id in [household_1.id, household_2.id]:\n        other_group_repos = I’m drafting the plan around four areas that need early proof: preserving schema behavior while removing ORM helpers, keeping tenant scoping intact, matching query and search results, and retaining the historical backup tests.
